Chikungunya Virus Explore This Topic Search Search Clear Search For Everyone About Symptoms Causes and Spread Vaccines Prevention Areas at Risk View all Health Care Providers Clinical Signs and Symptoms Vaccine Product Information Clinical Testing and Diagnosis Treatment and Prevention View all Public Health Resources Mosquito Control View all search clear search Chikungunya Virus Menu clear search For Everyone About Symptoms Causes and Spread Vaccines Prevention Areas at Risk View All Health Care Providers Clinical Signs and Symptoms Vaccine Product Information Clinical Testing and Diagnosis Treatment and Prevention View All Public Health Resources Mosquito Control View All Related Topics Vector-Borne Diseases Clinical Signs and Symptoms Vaccine Product Information Clinical Testing and Diagnosis Treatment and Prevention View Menu Clinical Signs and Symptoms of Chikungunya Virus Disease For Health Care Providers Oct. 21, 2026, visit link for details. Key points Chikungunya virus disease should be suspected in patients with acute fever and polyarthralgia and recent travel to an area with chikungunya virus. Several other viruses, including dengue, should be considered in the differential diagnosis depending on travel history and exposures. Rare and serious complications of chikungunya virus disease can occur, particularly among certain risk groups. Disease is usually self-limited, but some patients experience persistent arthralgia for months to years. Clinical presentation Chikungunya virus disease should be considered in patients with acute onset of fever and polyarthralgia, especially travelers who recently returned from areas with known virus transmission. The differential diagnosis varies based on place of residence, travel history, and exposures. Dengue, Zika, and chikungunya viruses are transmitted by the same mosquitoes and have similar clinical features. These viruses can circulate in the same area and can cause occasional co-infections in the same patient. Chikungunya virus infection is more likely to cause high fever, severe arthralgia, arthritis, rash, and lymphopenia, while dengue virus infection is more likely to cause neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, hemorrhage, shock, and death. In general, the signs and symptoms of Zika virus are mild, though infections can cause congenital infections. It is important to rule out dengue virus infection because proper clinical management of dengue can improve outcome. Keep Reading Clinical Features of Dengue In addition to dengue and Zika, other diagnostic considerations include leptospirosis, malaria, rickettsia, group A streptococcus, rubella, measles, parvovirus, enteroviruses, adenovirus, other alphavirus infections (Mayaro, Ross River, Barmah Forest, o’nyong-nyong, and Sindbis viruses), post-infectious arthritis, and rheumatologic conditions. Signs and symptoms The majority of people infected with chikungunya virus become symptomatic. The incubation period is typically 3–7 days (range, 1–12 days). The disease is most often characterized by acute onset of fever (typically >39°C [102°F]) and polyarthralgia. Joint symptoms are usually bilateral and symmetric and can be severe and debilitating. Other symptoms can include headache, myalgia, arthritis, conjunctivitis, nausea/vomiting, or maculopapular rash. Rare complications include uveitis, retinitis, myocarditis, hepatitis, nephritis, bullous skin lesions, hemorrhage, meningoencephalitis, myelitis, Guillain-Barré syndrome, and cranial nerve palsies. Persons at risk for severe disease include neonates exposed intrapartum or shortly after birth, older adults (>65 years), and persons with underlying medical conditions (e.g., hypertension, diabetes, or cardiovascular disease). Keep Reading Chikungunya Atypical and Severe Disease Manifestations Clinical assessment Clinical laboratory findings can include lymphopenia, thrombocytopenia, and elevated creatinine. Elevated hepatic transaminases can be seen with acute infection but are not specific and do not occur frequently enough to be diagnostic. Outcomes Acute symptoms typically resolve within 7–10 days. Some patients may have persistence or relapse of rheumatologic symptoms (polyarthralgia, polyarthritis, tenosynovitis) in the months following acute illness. Studies report variable proportions of patients with persistent joint pains for months to years. Mortality is rare and occurs mostly in older adults. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

MHRA 撤销 IXCHIQ 基孔肯雅病 疫苗 的营销授权 - 药品业务

MHRA withdraws marketing authorisation for IXCHIQ chikungunya vaccine - Pharmacy Business
新闻简报药品和保健品监管机构 (MHRA) 已撤销 IXCHIQ 基孔肯雅热 疫苗 的营销授权,自 2026 年 10 月 21 日起生效。这一决定是在对授权后安全数据进行审查并与人类药物委员会 (CHM) 协商后做出的,该委员会得出的结论是,疫苗 的利益-风险平衡不再有利。MHRA 建议医疗保健专业人员不要使用 疫苗 并处置任何剩余库存。他们还应告知旅行者其他预防措施,例如避免蚊虫叮咬和遵循当前的旅行健康建议。据报道,有严重的不良反应,包括基孔肯雅热样症状和脑炎等罕见的神经系统事件,有些病例导致住院或死亡。英国目前只有一种获得批准的基孔肯雅病毒 疫苗,即 Vimkunya,由 Bavarian Nordic 开发。已收到 IXCHIQ 疫苗 的个人无需采取进一步行动,因为不需要额外的医疗 治疗。MHRA 还敦促更新所有材料和系统,以删除对 IXCHIQ 疫苗 的引用。
查看新闻五要素
时间
MHRA 于 2026 年 10 月 21 日撤回了 IXCHIQ 基孔肯雅热 疫苗 的营销授权。该决定是在审查授权后安全数据并与人类药物委员会 (CHM) 协商后做出的。
地点与对象
英国是地点,受影响的人群包括前往已知基孔肯雅热 传播 的国家的个人,以及医疗保健专业人员和公众。
事件
出于对利益-风险平衡的担忧,MHRA 撤回了 IXCHIQ 基孔肯雅热 疫苗 的营销授权。该决定是基于人类药物委员会 (CHM) 的建议。
规模、影响与风险
自IXCHIQ获得授权以来,已有严重不良反应报告,包括基孔肯雅热样症状和脑炎等罕见的神经系统事件。有些病例导致住院或死亡。MHRA 建议医疗保健专业人员考虑采取替代措施来预防基孔肯雅热感染。
应对、状态与不确定性
MHRA 建议医疗保健专业人员不要开处方、供应或管理 IXCHIQ 疫苗 并处置任何剩余库存。他们还应更新材料和通讯,以删除对 IXCHIQ 的引用。该决定基于授权后安全数据和 CHM 咨询。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

MHRA withdraws marketing authorisation for IXCHIQ chikungunya vaccine - Pharmacy Business

News Brief
The Medicines and Healthcare products Regulatory Agency (MHRA) has withdrawn the marketing authorisation for the IXCHIQ chikungunya vaccine, effective from 2026-10-21. This decision follows a review of post-authorisation safety data and consultation with the Commission on Human Medicines (CHM), which concluded that the benefit-risk balance for the vaccine is no longer favourable. The MHRA has advised healthcare professionals not to administer the vaccine and to dispose of any remaining stock. They should also inform travellers of alternative prevention measures, such as avoiding mosquito bites and following current travel health advice. Serious adverse reactions, including chikungunya-like symptoms and rare neurological events like encephalitis, have been reported, with some cases resulting in hospitalisation or death. The UK now has only one approved chikungunya vaccine, Vimkunya, developed by Bavarian Nordic. Individuals who have already received the IXCHIQ vaccine do not need further action, as no additional medical treatment is required. The MHRA also urged updating all materials and systems to remove references to the IXCHIQ vaccine.

Chikungunya By Shajil KumarOct 21, 2026 Shajil Kumar Shajil Kumar is a senior journalist at Pharmacy Business, where he covers daily news, features and investigations and keeps a close eye on the pharmacy sector's biggest stories. With nearly three decades in print and online journalism spanning general and financial news, he brings that depth of experience from his earlier roles at Asian Media Group's sister publications Eastern Eye and India Weekly See Full Bio Key Summary Healthcare professionals should advise travellers of alternative measures to prevent chikungunya infection, including other vaccines. IXCHIQ is a live attenuated vaccine and became available in the UK from June 2025. The UK now has only has one approved chikungunya vaccine - Vimkunya, developed by Bavarian Nordic. The MHRA has withdrawn the marketing authorisation for IXCHIQ chikungunya vaccine following the advice from the Commission on Human Medicines (CHM). The regulator said the benefit-risk balance for the IXCHIQ chikungunya vaccine is no longer favourable and has advised healthcare professionals to consider alternatives. IXCHIQ is a live attenuated vaccine, manufactured by Valneva, and became available in the UK from June 2025. The MHRA has advised healthcare professionals not to prescribe, supply or administer the IXCHIQ vaccine, and dispose of any remaining stock in accordance with local policies. They should advise travellers of alternative measures to prevent chikungunya infection, including other vaccines, avoidance of mosquito bites and adherence to current health-related travel advice. The UK now has only has one approved chikungunya vaccine - Vimkunya, a single-dose vaccine developed by Bavarian Nordic. They should also ensure that any materials, systems or communications referring to available UK vaccination options are updated to remove the IXCHIQ vaccine. Individuals who have already received the IXCHIQ vaccine do not need another vaccination or any other specific medical treatment because of this decision. Those experiencing suspected side effect following the vaccination should seek medical advice. Since the authorisation of IXCHIQ, serious adverse reactions have been reported, including chikungunya-like symptoms and rare neurological events such as encephalitis. Most of these cases involved older adults and individuals with underlying medical conditions were at greater risk. However, some individuals with no known health conditions were also affected. Some cases resulted in hospitalisation or death. After reviewing the post-authorisation safety data and consulting the CHM, the MHRA has decided to withdraw marketing authorisation for the IXCHIQ vaccine. Individuals travelling from the UK to regions with known chikungunya transmission are advised to consider alternative measures. Chikungunya virus is found in the subtropical regions of the Americas, Africa, Southeast Asia, India, and the Pacific Region. The MHRA had on 11 February this year curtailed the usage of IXCHIQ vaccine for adults over the age of 60 years, and all individuals with hypertension, cardiovascular disease, diabetes mellitus, and/or chronic kidney disease. Chikungunya symptoms The chikungunya virus is transmitted by the bite of an infected female Aedes mosquito and the symptoms include sudden onset of fever usually accompanied by joint pain. The joint pain may be very debilitating, but usually ends within a few days or weeks. However, in some cases, joint pain and arthritis may persist for several months or even years. mhracommission on human medicines (chm)ixchiq vaccinecardiovascular diseasechronic kidney diseasediabeteschikungunya vaccine Related News News PRAG unveils plan to boost research in pharmacy Shajil Kumar 1h News Three get jail for £279k fraud at Worcestershire Acute Hospitals NHS Trust Shajil Kumar 2h News CPPE to conduct dermatology workshop for pharmacists, pharmacy technicians Shajil Kumar 6h News RCPharm's Barnett Award for Heather Smith Shajil Kumar 7h

Time
The MHRA withdrew the marketing authorisation for IXCHIQ chikungunya vaccine on 2026-10-21. The decision followed a review of post-authorisation safety data and consultation with the Commission on Human Medicines (CHM).
Location and population
The UK is the location, and the affected population includes individuals travelling to countries with known chikungunya transmission, as well as healthcare professionals and the general public.
Event
The MHRA withdrew the marketing authorisation for the IXCHIQ chikungunya vaccine due to concerns about the benefit-risk balance. This decision was based on the advice from the Commission on Human Medicines (CHM).
Scale, impact and risk
Since the authorisation of IXCHIQ, serious adverse reactions have been reported, including chikungunya-like symptoms and rare neurological events such as encephalitis. Some cases resulted in hospitalisation or death. The MHRA advised healthcare professionals to consider alternative measures to prevent chikungunya infection.
Response, status and uncertainty
The MHRA has advised healthcare professionals not to prescribe, supply, or administer the IXCHIQ vaccine and to dispose of any remaining stock. They should also update materials and communications to remove references to IXCHIQ. The decision was based on post-authorisation safety data and CHM consultation.

洪都拉斯确认基孔肯雅热在当地传播

Honduras Confirms Local Chikungunya Spread
新闻简报洪都拉斯已确诊八例基孔肯雅热病例,这是一种由蚊子传播的病毒,会导致严重的关节疼痛和发烧,据报道尼加拉瓜边境附近的一个村庄埃尔佩德雷加利托有局部传播。第一例输入性病例是一名 31 岁的卡车司机,于 2026 年 9 月 4 日从马那瓜返回,登革热检测结果呈阴性。卫生部通过其卫生部门负责人 监测 霍默·梅希亚 (Homer Mejía) 指出,该病毒目前正在该国境内人与人之间传播。疾病预防控制中心已向尼加拉瓜发布了 2 级旅行健康通知,但未向洪都拉斯发布了 2 级旅行健康通知。在审查的来源中尚未报告基孔肯雅热造成的死亡。暴发 仍然开放,边境村庄受影响的人数尚未披露。洪都拉斯公立医院因拖欠工资而停工 48 天后,将于 2026 年 10 月 26 日恢复运营。该部已在受影响地区采取蚊虫控制措施。暴发 与更广泛的区域背景相关,泛美卫生组织指出该病毒已在中美洲重新出现,并敦促加强 暴发 和卫生服务准备。应对措施包括熏蒸、杀幼虫剂的应用和公共卫生咨询。
查看新闻五要素
时间
洪都拉斯卫生部于 2026 年 10 月 23 日星期五确认了 8 例基孔肯雅热病例。第一例输入性病例于 2026 年 9 月 4 日星期五得到确认。暴发 仍在进行中,截至 2026 年 10 月 23 日星期五,阿劳卡的埃尔佩德雷加利托报告有当地传播。疾病预防控制中心于 2026 年 9 月 3 日星期四更新了尼加拉瓜的 2 级旅行健康通知。暴发仍然开放,边境村庄的患病人数不公开。
地点与对象
暴发 事件发生在洪都拉斯阿劳卡市的一个村庄埃尔佩德雷加利托,靠近尼加拉瓜边境。受影响人口包括边境村庄和首都地区、亚特兰蒂达、埃尔帕拉伊索和奥兰乔的居民。该病毒由伊蚊传播。
事件
洪都拉斯卫生部确认了八例经实验室确诊的基孔肯雅热病例,其中包括阿劳卡州埃尔佩德雷加利托的本地传播群。首例输入病例是一名从尼加拉瓜马那瓜回国的31岁卡车司机。该部报告了该国当地的 传播,在边境村庄的家庭中发现了症状。
规模、影响与风险
报告了八例实验室确诊病例,其中四例在首都地区,两例在亚特兰蒂斯达,埃尔帕拉伊索和奥兰乔各一例。阿劳卡省埃尔佩德雷加利托市已确认出现本地传播。疾病预防控制中心列出了尼加拉瓜的 2 级旅行健康通知,但没有洪都拉斯的通知。在审查的来源中尚未报告基孔肯雅热造成的死亡。
应对、状态与不确定性
洪都拉斯卫生部证实了这些病例,并报告了当地的传播情况。快速反应小组在首例输入性病例居住地区进行了熏蒸和喷洒杀幼虫剂。疾病预防控制中心建议前往尼加拉瓜的旅行者采取预防措施。洪都拉斯公立医院因拖欠工资而停工 48 天,将于 2026 年 10 月 26 日星期一恢复运营。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Honduras Confirms Local Chikungunya Spread

News Brief
Honduras has confirmed eight cases of chikungunya, a mosquito-borne virus causing severe joint pain and fever, with local spread reported in El Pedregalito, a village near the Nicaraguan border. The first imported case was a 31-year-old trucker returning from Managua on 4 September 2026, after a negative dengue test. The health ministry, through its head of health surveillance, Homer Mejía, noted that the virus is now spreading between people within the country. The CDC has issued a Level 2 travel health notice for Nicaragua but not for Honduras. No deaths from chikungunya have been reported in the sources reviewed. The outbreak remains open, with the number of affected individuals in the border village not disclosed. Public hospitals in Honduras are resuming operations on 26 October 2026 after a 48-day stoppage due to unpaid salaries. The ministry has initiated mosquito control measures in the affected area. The outbreak is linked to the broader regional context, with PAHO noting the virus has reappeared in Central America and urging strengthened surveillance and health service preparedness. The response includes fumigation, larvicide application, and public health advisories.

Lee, public domain, via Wikimedia Commons) HONDURAS · HEALTH · MOSQUITO-BORNE VIRUS Honduras’s health ministry has confirmed eight cases of chikungunya, a mosquito-borne virus that causes severe joint pain, including a cluster that is spreading locally in a border village near Nicaragua. Key Facts — The count Eight lab-confirmed cases: four in the capital district, two in Atlántida, one each in El Paraíso and Olancho. — The cluster Local spread in El Pedregalito, Alauca, about 5 km (3 miles) from the Nicaraguan border. — First import A 31-year-old trucker back from Managua was the first imported case of 2026, confirmed Friday 4 September. — The US link Neighbouring Nicaragua carries a Level 2 US health notice for chikungunya, updated 3 September. — Still open How many people are sick in the border village is not public. Honduras’s health ministry has confirmed eight cases of chikungunya, a mosquito-borne virus that causes severe joint pain, and says the virus is now spreading between people inside the country. The cluster is in El Pedregalito, a village in the municipality of Alauca about 5 kilometres (3 miles) from the Nicaraguan border, Homer Mejía, the ministry’s head of health surveillance, said on Friday 23 October. For US readers the news matters because chikungunya can be caught on trips to Central America, and the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) already lists a Level 2 travel health notice for neighbouring Nicaragua. The CDC’s notices page listed no chikungunya notice for Honduras on Sunday morning, 25 October. In Plain English Chikungunya: a viral disease spread by Aedes mosquitoes that causes fever and severe joint pain. Deaths are rare, according to the CDC. a viral disease spread by Aedes mosquitoes that causes fever and severe joint pain. Deaths are rare, according to the CDC. Local (community) spread: people are being infected inside Honduras by local mosquitoes, not only after trips abroad. people are being infected inside Honduras by local mosquitoes, not only after trips abroad. Sesal: Honduras’s Secretaría de Salud, the health ministry that runs public hospitals and tracks disease outbreaks. Honduras’s Secretaría de Salud, the health ministry that runs public hospitals and tracks disease outbreaks. PAHO: the Pan American Health Organization, the World Health Organization’s regional office for the Americas. What Honduras Has Confirmed Mejía said that laboratory tests have confirmed eight cases so far this year: four in the capital district, two in Atlántida on the Caribbean coast, one in El Paraíso and one in Olancho. The first case, he said, was a Honduran from the municipality of Tela who travelled to Nicaragua and returned with symptoms. The finding that worries officials most is the local spread in El Pedregalito, where Mejía said families with symptoms have already been found. He said movement between Honduras and Nicaragua may be helping the virus travel, because people bitten by infected mosquitoes in Nicaragua can fall ill after they come home. Symptoms usually start three to seven days after a bite and include fever above 38 degrees Celsius (100.4 Fahrenheit), severe joint pain, weakness, fatigue and a rash, Mejía explained. There is no specific treatment for the virus, and care focuses on easing symptoms. Free download · Expat checklist Get the free checklist as PDF Moving to Honduras 2026: the step-by-step checklist. Download the PDF checklist How the Outbreak Reached Honduras The ministry announced the year’s first imported case on Friday 4 September: a 31-year-old truck driver who travels to Managua every three weeks and came back with symptoms like dengue’s. A dengue test was negative and a molecular test then confirmed chikungunya, after which a rapid-response team fumigated and applied a biological larvicide in the area where he lives. How the Outbreak Built Up Thursday 3 September: the CDC updates its Level 2 travel health notice for the chikungunya outbreak in Nicaragua. the CDC updates its Level 2 travel health notice for the chikungunya outbreak in Nicaragua. Friday 4 September: Honduras confirms its first imported case of 2026, a trucker returning from Managua. Honduras confirms its first imported case of 2026, a trucker returning from Managua. Tuesday 20 October: PAHO publishes an update on dengue and chikungunya in the Americas. PAHO publishes an update on dengue and chikungunya in the Americas. Friday 23 October: the ministry reports eight confirmed cases and local spread in Alauca. the ministry reports eight confirmed cases and local spread in Alauca. Monday 26 October: public hospital clinics are due to reopen after 48 days of doctors’ assemblies. PAHO’s update counts 213,919 suspected chikungunya cases in the Americas through epidemiological week 38 of 2026, of which 78,481 were confirmed, with 88 deaths in 20 countries and territories. It says the virus has reappeared in Central American countries, but it gives no chikungunya figures for Honduras. Dengue and Hospitals Add Pressure The same Aedes mosquitoes spread dengue, and PAHO counts 9,782 dengue cases in Honduras through epidemiological week 37 in mid-September, including 1,083 confirmed cases and four deaths. Reports have stayed above 400 a week since week 30, with the Central District and Cortés, the department that includes San Pedro Sula, reporting the most. PAHO urged countries on Wednesday 21 October to strengthen surveillance and prepare health services for more dengue and chikungunya cases, noting that all four dengue virus types have circulated in the Americas in 2026. It linked the warning to an expected intensification of El Niño. Honduras’s public hospitals are also coming off a long stoppage, because outpatient consultations and scheduled surgeries are due to resume on Monday 26 October after 48 days of doctors’ assemblies over unpaid salaries, according to the Colegio Médico de Honduras (CMH). The government agreed on Friday night, 23 October, to widen a technical table to settle the payments, although the deal was still to be signed on Saturday morning. What It Means for You The CDC has issued no chikungunya notice for Honduras, but its Level 2 notice for Nicaragua gives the standard advice: prevent mosquito bites with repellent, long sleeves and trousers, and screened or air-conditioned rooms. That notice also recommends vaccination for travellers to an outbreak area and tells pregnant travellers to reconsider travel to affected areas, so anyone with a trip planned can ask a doctor or travel clinic. What the Honduran Health Ministry Advises Fever with severe joint pain: go to a health facility. go to a health facility. Medicines: avoid self-medicating, especially with aspirin or ibuprofen, until a doctor rules out dengue. avoid self-medicating, especially with aspirin or ibuprofen, until a doctor rules out dengue. At home: remove standing water where the mosquito breeds and reduce bites. The reports describe cases in the capital district and on the north coast, and none says that a tourist or foreign resident has been infected. They do not mention the Bay Islands or any other tourist destination. For Honduran families in the United States, the ministry’s advice in the box above applies to relatives at home as well. Pregnant women, older adults, babies under one year and people with chronic illness are the highest-risk groups. What Is Not Known The number of people ill in El Pedregalito has not been published, and Mejía described only families with symptoms near the border. It is also not known how many of the other seven cases caught the virus abroad, because the reports say only that the first case was imported, and they do not say whether it is the 31-year-old trucker announced on 4 September. The ministry’s homepage carried no bulletin on the outbreak on Sunday 25 October, and PAHO’s update of 20 October has no chikungunya data for Honduras. The reports do not say how many mosquito-control teams are working in Alauca. No death from chikungunya has been reported in the sources reviewed. The reports also do not say how many suspected cases are waiting for laboratory results or how many patients are in hospital. More: Latin America news in English, every day from The Rio Times. Frequently Asked Questions What is chikungunya? Chikungunya is a viral disease spread by Aedes mosquitoes that causes fever and severe joint pain. Most people recover within a week, but joint pain can last for months, and deaths are rare, the CDC says. How many cases has Honduras confirmed? The health ministry has confirmed eight cases this year, four of them in the capital district. It reports local spread in El Pedregalito, Alauca, near the Nicaraguan border. Is it safe to travel to Honduras? The CDC lists no chikungunya notice for Honduras on 25 October, though it has a Level 2 notice for Nicaragua. Its standard advice is to prevent mosquito bites with repellent, covering clothes and screened rooms. How does chikungunya spread? Mosquitoes of the Aedes genus spread it, the same insects that spread dengue. The ministry says the first case was brought in by a Honduran who had travelled to Nicaragua, and that the virus now passes between people locally. Is there a treatment? There is no antiviral treatment, and care focuses on easing symptoms. Honduran officials advise seeing a clinic for fever with joint pain and avoiding aspirin and ibuprofen until dengue is excluded. 血液样本显示住院患者的基孔肯雅热检测时间更长 - Bioengineer.org

Blood samples showed longer chikungunya detection in hospitalized patients - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报中国南宁的一项研究发现,在 2025 年 暴发 期间,住院患者的血液样本检测到 基孔肯雅病毒 的一致性比唾液或尿液的 基孔肯雅病毒 的检测结果更一致,持续时间也更长。血清和血浆的阳性率最高,而唾液和尿液的阳性率较低。这支持血液作为检测 基孔肯雅病毒 并估计其在体内持久性的首选样本类型。
查看新闻五要素
时间
研究人员收集了 506 份体液样本,发现大多数样本中血清和血浆呈阳性,而唾液和尿液中的阳性率要低得多。
地点与对象
中国广西省南宁市。
事件
与唾液和尿液相比,血液样本显示住院患者的基孔肯雅热检测时间更长。
规模、影响与风险
研究发现,血液样本中 基孔肯雅病毒 的检测结果比唾液或尿液更一致、持续时间更长,其中血清和血浆的阳性率最高。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究对2025年中国南宁基孔肯雅热暴发期间的53名住院患者进行了纵向分析,比较了不同样本类型中检测结果呈阳性的概率。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Blood samples showed longer chikungunya detection in hospitalized patients - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study in Nanning, China, found that blood samples detected chikungunya virus more consistently and for longer than saliva or urine in hospitalized patients during the 2025 outbreak. Serum and plasma had the highest positivity rates, while saliva and urine showed lower rates. This supports blood as the preferred sample type for detecting chikungunya virus and estimating its persistence in the body.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Thursday, October 22, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Blood samples showed longer chikungunya detection in hospitalized patients by October 22, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 Blood samples showed longer chikungunya detection in hospitalized patients Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Blood samples detected chikungunya virus more consistently and for longer than saliva or urine in a longitudinal study of 53 hospitalized patients in Nanning, China, during the country’s largest recorded chikungunya outbreak in 2025. Researchers collected 506 body-fluid specimens and found that serum and plasma were positive in most samples, whereas positivity was substantially lower in saliva and urine. The findings indicate that blood was the more informative specimen type for assessing detection during acute illness, although the results are limited to a hospitalized cohort and should not automatically be applied to people with mild or asymptomatic infection. Chikungunya virus is an acute mosquito-borne infection transmitted by Aedes mosquitoes. The study followed hospitalized patients with symptomatic disease and compared the probability of positive test results in serum, plasma, saliva and urine at different points after symptom onset. Serum had the highest observed positivity rate, at 81.20%, followed by plasma at 72.50%. Saliva was positive in 11.03% of samples and urine in 4.73%. Discover more Find Treatments Study Anatomy Biology textbook sales Investigators also compared cycle-threshold, or Ct, values between specimen types. During the first four days after symptoms began, median Ct values were lower in serum and plasma than in saliva and urine. The analysis therefore found stronger and more consistent detection in blood specimens early in illness. The study used these laboratory values to compare the timing of detection across specimen types; it did not use them to establish how much infectious virus was present in any sample. Discover more Check Symptoms Get Health Tips Study Biology Book Lab Tests A generalized estimating equations model was used to estimate how the probability of a positive result changed with the number of days since symptom onset. The sharpest decline occurred in saliva: its estimated probability of testing positive fell from 52.2% on day 1 to 0.7% by day 7. Blood positivity declined more gradually. Serum and plasma positivity rates were both above 85% on day 3 and were approximately 30% by day 7. Urine remained at a low positivity level throughout, and the number of days since symptom onset was not significantly associated with a positive urine result. These are modeled probabilities based on the study’s observed specimens, rather than measurements of infectious virus or proof that a person remained contagious for the same duration. The models also describe the study population and sampling schedule, so the day-specific estimates should not be interpreted as universal values for every patient or every specimen collection method. The relatively low number of positive urine samples further limited the precision of conclusions about that fluid. Parametric survival analysis provided additional estimates of the duration of detection. The median duration was 5.8 days in serum, with a 95% confidence interval of 4.8 to 6.9 days, and also 5.8 days in plasma, with a 95% confidence interval of 4.7 to 7.1 days. Saliva had a shorter median detection period of 3.5 days, with a 95% confidence interval of 3.0 to 4.1 days. Urine was excluded from this analysis because too few urine samples tested positive to support the model. Taken together, the results show that blood specimens retained detectable chikungunya virus longer and with greater consistency than the non-blood fluids examined. Saliva performed better than urine for detecting the virus during the acute phase, but its modeled positivity fell quickly. The findings support blood as the preferred sample type for detecting chikungunya virus and estimating how long detection persisted in the body during the period studied. They do not establish the duration of viable virus, whether transmission could occur from any specimen, or whether the same patterns occur outside hospitalized patients. Discover more Book Care Services Read Med Journals infections infection infectious The study was conducted in a single city during the 2025 outbreak, and its hospitalized population may not represent the wider range of chikungunya infections. Repeated specimens were available for 53 patients, but the analysis was limited to the body-fluid samples collected from that cohort and to the laboratory tests performed. Within those limits, the results provide a specimen-focused account of chikungunya detection dynamics during acute disease and identify differences between blood, saliva and urine that may help frame subsequent surveillance and public health control work. Subject of Research: Longitudinal analysis of chikungunya virus detection and persistence in serum, plasma, saliva and urine among hospitalized patients in Nanning, China. Time
Researchers collected 506 body-fluid specimens and found that serum and plasma were positive in most samples, whereas positivity was substantially lower in saliva and urine.
Location and population
Nanning, Guangxi Province, China.
Event
Blood samples showed longer chikungunya detection in hospitalized patients compared to saliva and urine.
Scale, impact and risk
The study found that blood samples detected chikungunya virus more consistently and for longer than saliva or urine, with serum and plasma having the highest positivity rates.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study used a longitudinal analysis of 53 hospitalized patients during the 2025 chikungunya outbreak in Nanning, China, to compare the probability of positive test results in different specimen types.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

UK revokes Valneva's chikungunya vaccine marketing approval - pharma.economictimes.indiatimes.com

News Brief
The UK's MHRA revoked Valneva's chikungunya vaccine, Ixchiq, due to safety concerns. Serious adverse reactions, including chikungunya-like symptoms and neurological events, were reported, with some cases resulting in hospitalisation or death. The vaccine will be withdrawn from the market, and healthcare professionals are prohibited from administering it.

Online Bureau ET Pharma Updated On Oct 22, 2026 at 05:58 PM IST London: The UK’s Medicines and Healthcare products Regulatory Agency (MHRA) has ordered the "withdrawal of the marketing authorisation" of Valneva’s chikungunya vaccine, Ixchiq. Following a post-authorisation safety review and suggestion by the Commission on Human Medicines, the UK’s medicines regulator said the benefit-risk balance of the vaccine was no longer considered favourable. Healthcare professionals have been prohibited from prescribing or administering the vaccine. Advt Patients who have previously received Ixchiq have been advised to take medical advice if they have experienced a suspected adverse reaction following vaccination. Ixchiq is single-dose live attenuated vaccine indicated for the prevention of chikungunya virus infection in adults travelling to high-risk areas. Since its authorisation, MHRA flagged that, "serious adverse reactions have been reported, including chikungunya-like symptoms and rare neurological events such as encephalitis." PAHO is calling on countries to strengthen epidemiological, virological and entomological surveillance, expand diagnostic capacity and ensure that cases are detected early and patients receive timely care. It also recommended stronger integrated measures to control Aedes mosquitoes, which transmit both diseases. Health services should be prepared to respond to potential increases in cases at all levels of care, PAHO said, with particular attention to strengthening primary healthcare workers’ ability to recognise dengue warning signs early and prevent progression to severe illness. Hospitals should also be ready to manage patients with warning signs and severe cases, the agency added. PAHO further urged countries to improve environmental management, remove containers that collect water and encourage communities, families, local authorities and other sectors to participate actively in mosquito prevention and control. 新闻简报2026 年《BMC 传染病》杂志上发表的一项系统综述发现,非洲各地的埃博拉病毒病和基孔肯雅热幸存者在急性感染后经常会出现持续数周至数年的持续症状。该综述分析了截至 2025 年 11 月 7 日的研究,确定了广泛的 患病率 估计值,基孔肯雅热为 12.1% 至 78.6%,埃博拉为 17.5% 至 98.6%。持续症状包括肌肉骨骼疼痛、眼部并发症、头痛、疲劳和 关节痛。作者强调了非洲对幸存者进行长期护理和调整卫生系统的必要性,并指出大多数研究中缺乏比较组,使明确的因果归因变得复杂。他们还强调了了解这些对健康公平和全球卫生安全的长期后果的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年发表的系统综述,截至 2025 年 11 月 7 日收集的证据。
地点与对象
非洲,重点关注埃博拉病毒病和基孔肯雅热的幸存者。
事件
一项系统评价发现,埃博拉病毒病和基孔肯雅热的幸存者在急性感染后经常会出现持续数周至数年的持续症状。该评论发表在《BMC 传染病》杂志上。
规模、影响与风险
患病率 对持续症状的估计差异很大,基孔肯雅热的持续症状为 12.1% 至 78.6%,埃博拉为 17.5% 至 98.6%。持续症状包括肌肉骨骼疼痛、眼部并发症、头痛、疲劳和 关节痛。该审查强调了长期幸存者护理和卫生系统调整的必要性。
应对、状态与不确定性
由于研究之间存在很大的异质性,该综述已在开放科学框架上注册并进行了描述性总结。作者警告说,大多数研究中缺乏对照组,这使得将症状归因于感染本身变得复杂。这些发现为未来的有针对性的研究提供了一个信号。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Persistent symptoms common after Ebola and chikungunya across Africa, review finds - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A systematic review published in BMC Infectious Diseases in 2026 found that survivors of Ebola virus disease and chikungunya across Africa frequently experience persistent symptoms lasting weeks to years after their acute infection. The review, which analyzed studies up to 7 November 2025, identified a wide range of prevalence estimates, from 12.1% to 78.6% for chikungunya and 17.5% to 98.6% for Ebola. Persistent symptoms included musculoskeletal pain, ocular complications, headache, fatigue, and arthralgia. The authors emphasized the need for long-term survivor care and adaptation of health systems in Africa, noting that the lack of comparison groups in most studies complicates definitive causal attributions. They also highlighted the importance of understanding these chronic consequences for health equity and global health security.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, October 24, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Persistent symptoms common after Ebola and chikungunya across Africa, review finds by October 24, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 Persistent symptoms common after Ebola and chikungunya across Africa, review finds Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Survivors of Ebola virus disease and chikungunya across Africa frequently experience persistent symptoms lasting weeks to years after their acute infection, according to a systematic review published in BMC Infectious Diseases. The review examined post-acute infection syndromes following non-COVID infections, a family of conditions that has gained global attention through long COVID. Although many pathogens most closely linked to these syndromes are endemic to Africa, evidence from the continent has remained scarce. The peer-reviewed article is being shared early to provide faster access and will be replaced by the final Version of Record. Researchers searched Medline, Embase and grey literature up to 7 November 2025 for studies conducted in Africa that reported symptoms four or more weeks after acute infection. Case reports, case series, conference abstracts, secondary research, comments and correspondence were excluded. Two reviewers independently screened the records and extracted data on study characteristics, symptom prevalence, clinical presentation and associated factors. Because substantial heterogeneity was observed across the included studies, the findings were summarised descriptively rather than pooled statistically, and the review was registered on the Open Science Framework. Of 1,368 records, 65 studies were included, most carrying a moderate to low risk of bias. Forty-two studies examined Ebola virus disease and 19 examined chikungunya. The authors describe the evidence base as scarce, heterogeneous and geographically concentrated, and note that many pathogens most closely linked to post-acute infection syndromes are endemic to Africa, a gap they frame as having implications for both health equity and global health security. Discover more Check Symptoms Study Biology Track Outbreaks Infectious Diseases infections Prevalence estimates varied widely. Between 17.5 percent and 98.6 percent of Ebola survivors reported persistent symptoms, while the range after chikungunya stretched from 12.1 percent to 78.6 percent. The authors refrained from combining these estimates into a single headline prevalence, summarising the results descriptively in view of the substantial heterogeneity across studies. Despite the spread of figures, the reported prevalence of persistent symptoms among survivors was high. The clinical picture differed between the two diseases. After Ebola, musculoskeletal pain, ocular complications, headache and fatigue predominated. After chikungunya, arthralgia was the hallmark of post-chikungunya disease. Across the included studies, a substantial symptom burden persisted years after the acute infection. Across studies, three factors emerged most frequently as being associated with persistent sequelae: female sex, older age and greater severity of the acute illness. The review reports these as the factors most frequently associated with persistent sequelae in the included studies, not as proven causes of persistent illness. An exploratory, non-systematic supplementary search additionally identified sex-specific consequences among female survivors, including menstrual irregularities and adverse pregnancy outcomes. Because this supplementary component was not conducted systematically, the authors present it as a signal for future targeted research rather than a firm estimate of how commonly these reproductive health complications occur. Discover more infection Find Treatments Health health Get Health Tips The review also highlights a fundamental interpretive limitation: most included studies lacked comparison groups. The authors caution that the absence of comparison groups in most studies complicates attribution of the persistent symptoms to the infection itself. The high reported prevalence is therefore best understood as a measure of the documented symptom burden among survivors rather than a definitive causal estimate. Despite these caveats, the authors argue that the implications for health systems are clear. Health systems in Africa must adapt to provide long-term survivor care, embedding follow-up into existing chronic care platforms. As pathogens endemic to the continent carry pandemic potential and expand geographically, the review concludes that understanding their chronic consequences is both a matter of health equity and an urgent global health security priority. (2026). Beyond long COVID: post-acute infection syndromes across Africa, a systematic review. PAHO urges Caribbean to prepare for possible rise in dengue, chikungunya - Caribbean National Weekly

News Brief
The Pan American Health Organization (PAHO) has issued a warning to Caribbean countries about the potential rise in dengue and chikungunya cases, urging them to prepare for the possibility. This alert was reported by Caribbean National Weekly on October 23, 2026. The evidence does not provide specific case counts, deaths, or detailed measures taken in response to the outbreak. The warning is based on general public health concerns rather than confirmed data. The Caribbean region is identified as the location, but no further population details are given. The risk level is not explicitly stated in the evidence, and the status of the response remains uncertain due to the lack of confirmed information.

一只蚊子和随雨而来的类似发烧 - 《新印度快报》

One mosquito and the look-alike fevers that arrive with the rains - The New Indian Express
新闻简报印度钦奈等受季风袭击的城市的卫生官员警告说,由于早期症状相似,很难区分登革热、基孔肯雅热和寨卡病毒。他们强调需要进行准确的血液检测,并强调需要立即住院治疗的警告信号,例如出血、持续呕吐或严重胃痛。对于在季风季节出现类似症状的人来说,这种风险很高,因为误诊可能会导致并发症。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

One mosquito and the look-alike fevers that arrive with the rains - The New Indian Express

News Brief
Health officials in monsoon-hit cities like Chennai, India, warn about the difficulty in distinguishing between dengue, chikungunya, and Zika due to similar early symptoms. They emphasize the need for blood tests for accurate diagnosis and highlight warning signs such as bleeding, constant vomiting, or severe stomach pain that require immediate hospitalization. The risk is high for individuals experiencing similar symptoms during the monsoon season, as misdiagnosis can lead to complications.
Original source text (English)
Health One mosquito and the look-alike fevers that arrive with the rains Dengue, chikungunya and Zika are spread by the same mosquito and share similar symptoms in the early days. Here’s how to tell them apart, why a blood test is needed to confirm the infection, and which warning signs warrant an immediate hospital visit. When the rains arrive and water collects in pots, tyres and terraces, so do the mosquitoes. For many families in monsoon-hit cities like Chennai, the first sign of trouble is a fever that comes with body aches and a headache, and the first question is whether it’s dengue, chikungunya or something else. That is a fair question, because all three are spread by the same mosquito and can look almost identical in the first few days. We spoke to doctors about the clues that help tell them apart, why only a blood test can settle it, what to do at home, and the warning signs that mean heading to the hospital the same day. Why they are so hard to tell apart During seasons when mosquito-borne infections are prevalent, dengue, chikungunya and Zika can be difficult to distinguish because they often begin with similar symptoms, including fever, headache, muscle pain and body aches. However, according to Dr. Tushar Tayal, Associate Director, Internal Medicine, CK Birla Hospital, Gurugram, certain clinical features can help doctors identify the likely infection. Early days can be tricky. “Difference between Chikungunya and Zika in the first two days is hard to tell. Both Chikungunya and Zika come from the Aedes mosquito, the same one that spreads Dengue,” says Dr. Sushila Kataria, Vice Chairperson, Internal Medicine, Medanta Gurugram. “Dengue, chikungunya and Zika can look alike in the first few days, but Dengue is the one I worry about most, because platelets can drop quickly,” she adds. Dengue: severe body aches and falling platelets “Dengue may present with severe body aches, pain behind the eyes, a falling platelet count and, in some cases, warning signs such as abdominal pain, persistent vomiting or bleeding,” Dr. Tayal says. “Early assessment is particularly important in suspected Dengue, as the risk of complications can increase around the time the fever begins to settle,” he cautions. Chikungunya: the joints give it away “With chikungunya, the joints give it away,” says Dr. Kataria. “The fever comes on suddenly and is usually quite high, and then the pain sets in, mostly in the wrists, fingers, knees and ankles,” she explains. “Patients tell me they can’t open a bottle or climb stairs. In some people the stiffness stays for weeks after the fever is gone,” Dr. Kataria says. Dr. Tayal describes it in similar terms. “Chikungunya is more commonly associated with intense joint pain, swelling and stiffness, which may persist for weeks or even months after the fever subsides,” he says. Zika: usually mild, but not for everyone “Zika infection is often milder and may cause a characteristic rash, red eyes and joint pain, although many infected individuals experience few or no symptoms,” Dr. Tayal says. “Most people barely have symptoms while others may get a mild fever, red eyes and an itchy rash,” says Dr. Kataria. “But if a woman is pregnant, it needs to be taken seriously, because the virus can affect the baby’s brain. Any pregnant woman with fever and rash should get tested without waiting,” she cautions. Only a blood test can tell you which one it is The key is not to rely on symptoms alone. “A patient’s travel history, local outbreak patterns, day of illness and clinical examination all guide the diagnostic approach,” Dr. Tayal explains, adding that laboratory tests, including appropriate molecular or antibody tests depending on the stage of infection, help confirm the diagnosis. “Since the symptoms overlap, doctors rely on timing, symptom patterns and blood tests rather than guesswork,” Dr. Kataria says, advising that it is not a safe thing to guess which of the three one has, getting a blood test is. What to do at home According to the experts, drink plenty of fluids, and avoid painkillers like ibuprofen unless your doctor says so. Patients should avoid self-medication, particularly aspirin and ibuprofen until dengue has been ruled out, as these medicines can increase the risk of bleeding. Timely diagnosis and appropriate monitoring are essential to prevent complications and ensure effective management. When to head to the hospital the same day “If there’s bleeding, constant vomiting or severe stomach pain, go to the hospital the same day,” Dr. Kataria says. Dengue, chikungunya and Zika may look alike at the start, but one doesn’t have to stay clueless. If a fever and body aches show up during the rains, don’t wait it out or guess. Get a blood test, drink plenty of fluids and skip painkillers like ibuprofen unless your doctor says so. And if there is bleeding, constant vomiting or severe stomach pain, head to the hospital the same day. The New Indian Express www.newindianexpress.com

Original source text (Chinese)

请用最新版微博客户端扫码 扫描成功! 点击手机上的确认即可登录 自媒体热点 > 正文 行情 股吧 新闻 外汇 新三板 行情 行情 股吧 新闻 外汇 新三板 登革热VS基孔肯雅热:症状对比、传播途径和预防措施 登革热VS基孔肯雅热:症状对比、传播途径和预防措施 2026年10月26日 10:44 市场资讯 新浪财经APP举报 缩小字体 放大字体 收藏 微博 分享 MD 微信 腾讯QQ QQ空间 (来源:白沙融媒) 所谓“蚊媒传染病”,就是蚊子在叮咬时把病毒或寄生虫“快递”到人体内的疾病;国内需要重点盯防的有登革热、基孔肯雅热、寨卡病毒病、乙型脑炎和疟疾等。 今天,我们先锁定由伊蚊“投递”的两大热门疾病——登革热与基孔肯雅热。 01 什么是登革热? 它是由登革病毒引起的,经伊蚊叮咬传播的虫媒传染病。据世界卫生组织统计,全球每年约有1-4亿人感染,其中美洲、东南亚和西太平洋(3.350, 0.00, 0.00%)区域发病最为严重,亚洲约占全球疾病负担的70%。我国广东、云南、广西等多个省份曾多次发生本地疫情。 02 登革热的典型症状有哪些? 登革热的潜伏期(从被携带病毒的蚊子叮咬到症状开始的时间)一般为1-14天,多为5-9天。值得注意的是大多数感染者没有症状或症状轻微,不过,一旦发病,患者会出现一系列典型症状: 1.突发高热:体温可达40°C以上; 2.“三痛”:剧烈头痛、眼眶痛、全身肌肉关节痛; 3.“三红”:面部、颈部、胸部潮红; 4.皮疹:四肢或躯干出现充血性红疹; 5.消化道症状:恶心、呕吐、腹泻、食欲不振等消化道症状; 6.严重者:可发展为登革出血热或登革休克综合征,甚至危及生命。 03 什么是基孔肯雅热? 基孔肯雅热(Chikungunya fever)是一种由基孔肯雅病毒(CHIKV)引起,通过白纹伊蚊或埃及伊蚊传播的病毒性疾病。基孔肯雅热始发于非洲,最早于1952年在坦桑尼亚首次被发现。“基孔肯雅”一词就来源于坦桑尼亚南部基马孔德语,意思是“变得扭曲”,用以描述出现严重关节疼痛的感染者痛得弯腰、蜷缩身体的样子。 根据世界卫生组织公布的最新数据,截至2024年12月,全球119个国家和地区已报告基孔肯雅热本地传播。 近些年我国周边的东南亚疫情持续活跃。我国于2008年首次发现输入性病例,曾引发本地疫情,但尚未形成稳定的疫源地。2025年7月广东省佛山市发生疫情,经全力防控,现疫情已呈明显下降趋势。 04 基孔肯雅热有何症状? 被带基孔肯雅病毒的蚊子叮咬后,病毒在体内潜伏期通常为3-7天(最短1天,最长可达12天),随后可能突然发病,出现典型的“热-疹-痛”三联征: 🔺 突发高烧:体温可高达39°C以上,可能持续1-7天。部分患者会出现“双峰热”,即退烧后再次发热。 🔺 皮疹:约80%的患者在发热后2-5天,面部、四肢甚至手掌脚底会出现红色斑疹或丘疹,常伴有瘙痒。 🔺 明显关节疼痛:这是基孔肯雅热最突出的特点之一。特别是手指、手腕、脚踝和脚趾等小关节会出现剧烈疼痛和肿胀,可能持续数天甚至数月。 此外,还可能伴随头痛、恶心、呕吐、肌肉痛、结膜炎等症状。 05 这两种疾病有什么区别? 这两种疾病,各年龄人群普遍易感,发病初期都会出现高热、头痛、关节和肌肉疼痛等症状。 登革热发生重症的比例较高,致死病例时有发生。基孔肯雅热多数患者为轻症,大约会在1~2周内康复,但部分人(尤其是老年人和慢性病患者)可能会经历长期关节疼痛的折磨;只有极少数情况下,该病会导致出血、脑炎等严重并发症甚至死亡。 两种疾病的特征对比 在登革热和基孔肯雅热共同流行的地区,常有人同时感染两种病毒。 06 这两种疾病是如何传播的? 登革热和基孔肯雅热的传播途径相似,都是由白纹伊蚊和埃及伊蚊传播。伊蚊白天叮咬人,尤其活跃于日出后1-2小时和日落前2-3小时。它们的幼虫孳生于小型积水容器中,如瓶罐、废旧轮胎、花盆等,这些地方都可能成为它们的“温床”。 07 如何预防这两种疾病? 面对蚊媒传染病的威胁,我们可以通过科学有效的预防措施,降低被其感染的风险: 1.环境治理:伊蚊依赖小型积水繁殖,因此清除蚊虫孳生地是预防虫媒传染病的根本措施。我们需要定时清理家中容易积水的地方,如瓶罐、废旧轮胎、花盆、下水道、杂物堆等区域,不给蚊子提供繁殖的机会。 2.个人防护:外出时,尽量穿浅色长袖衣裤,减少皮肤暴露面积;同时在裸露皮肤上涂抹含避蚊胺、避蚊酯的驱蚊液。在家中,安装纱门纱窗,使用蚊帐,为自己打造一个安全的“无蚊空间”;必要时使用电蚊拍、灭蚊灯等,对蚊子进行“精准打击”。此外,尽量减少在户外树阴、草丛等蚊子多的地方逗留时间。 3.关注流行区疫情:如果计划前往东南亚、非洲等登革热和基孔肯雅热流行区,出行前一定要查询当地疫情信息,做好充分的驱蚊防蚊准备。归国后14天内做好健康监测,密切关注自己的身体状况。 4.及时就医:一旦出现发热、皮疹、关节痛等相关症状立即就医并告知旅行史和蚊虫叮咬史,以便医生能够及时准确地诊断和治疗。 海量资讯、精准解读,尽在新浪财经APP
